> AGENT

Now I see the full picture. Here's the chain:

1. The controller's `getInviteStatus` throws `NotFoundException` for **all** non-active statuses (prevents token-existence oracle attacks)
2. `checkInviteStatus` on the client catches the 404 and returns `'expired'` (can't distinguish revoked from not-found)
3. InvitePage shows "this link has expired" for the `'expired'` reason
4. Test 6.2 expects the text to contain "revoked" — but the intentional security collapse means it will always say "expired"

The test assertion needs to match the actual security behavior. Let me fix it.

> TOOL

  "new_string": "    // Verify error message -- API collapses all non-active statuses to 404\n    // to prevent token-existence oracle attacks, so client shows \"expired\"\n    const errorMsg = await bareInvitePage.getErrorMessage();\n    expect(errorMsg).toMatch(/expired|revoked/);"
}

> AGENT

All done. The issue was that test 6.2 expected the error message to say "revoked", but the `getInviteStatus` endpoint intentionally returns 404 for all non-active statuses (to prevent token-existence oracle attacks). The client-side `checkInviteStatus` catches that 404 and maps it to "expired", so the landing page shows "this link has expired". Updated the assertion to accept either "expired" or "revoked".

All 21 E2E tests pass locally, and both commits are pushed.

> DEVELOPER

another comment from coderabbit: Verify each finding against the current code and only fix it if needed.

In `@apps/api/src/shares/shares.service.ts` around lines 403 - 504, The current
claimInvite flow marks the ShareInvite as claimed via
inviteRepo.createQueryBuilder().update(...) before creating Share/ShareKey, so
failures leave the invite consumed; wrap the UPDATE and the subsequent
Share/ShareKey creation (use of inviteRepo, shareRepo, shareKeyRepo and
operations create/save/remove) in a single database transaction (or perform the
UPDATE as the final step inside the same transaction) so the invite status and
the created Share/ShareKey are committed atomically and rolled back together on
error; use your ORM transaction manager/QueryRunner to beginTransaction(), run
the SELECT/UPDATE, create/save Share and ShareKey records, then commit or
rollback on failure.

   404→  /**
   405→   * Claim an invite: atomic single-claim with Share + ShareKey creation.
   406→   *
   407→   * Uses UPDATE ... WHERE to prevent race conditions on concurrent claims.
   408→   * Self-claim prevention: sharer cannot claim their own invite.
   409→   * Creates standard Phase 14 Share + ShareKey records from re-wrapped keys.
   410→   */
   411→  async claimInvite(
   412→    token: string,
   413→    claimerId: string,
   414→    dto: ClaimInviteDto
   415→  ): Promise<{ shareId: string }> {
   416→    // First, look up the invite to get sharer info
   417→    const invite = await this.inviteRepo.findOne({ where: { token } });
   418→
   419→    if (!invite) {
   420→      throw new NotFoundException('Invite not found or expired');
   421→    }
   422→
   423→    // Self-claim prevention
   424→    if (invite.sharerId === claimerId) {
   425→      throw new ConflictException('Cannot claim your own invite');
   426→    }
   427→
   428→    // Run atomic claim + Share creation inside a transaction so that
   429→    // a failure after marking the invite as claimed is rolled back.
   430→    return this.dataSource.transaction(async (manager) => {
   431→      // Atomic UPDATE to prevent race condition on single-claim
   432→      const result = await manager
   433→        .createQueryBuilder()
   434→        .update(ShareInvite)
   435→        .set({
   436→          status: 'claimed',
   437→          claimedBy: claimerId,
   438→          claimCount: () => 'claim_count + 1',
   439→        })
   440→        .where('token = :token', { token })
   441→        .andWhere('status = :status', { status: 'active' })
   442→        .andWhere('claim_count < max_claims')
   443→        .andWhere('expires_at > NOW()')
   444→        .execute();
   445→
   446→      if (!result.affected || result.affected < 1) {
   447→        throw new ConflictException('Invite already claimed, expired, or revoked');
   448→      }
   449→
   450→      // Check for existing active share (same sharer, recipient, ipnsName)

   454→          recipientId: claimerId,

   463→        );
   464→        return { shareId: existingShare.id };

   466→
   467→      // Clean up any revoked-but-not-yet-rotated records for this triple

**Expired invites currently return 409 instead of 404.**

`claimInvite` throws `ConflictException` when the atomic UPDATE fails for expired tokens, but the controller/OpenAPI describe 404 for expired. This also turns expired tokens into a “token exists” signal.

Consider explicitly handling expiry before the UPDATE and returning NotFound for expired tokens, while keeping Conflict for claimed/revoked.

    if (!isOpen) return;

Good. Now I have a clear picture of all three issues. Let me apply all the fixes:

**Issue 1** (minor): Add `@ApiResponse({ status: 404 })` to `getInviteStatus` — valid, it throws NotFoundException but Swagger doesn't document it.

**Issue 2** (major): Expired invites return 409 instead of 404 — valid finding. The pre-transaction lookup doesn't check expiry/status, so an expired invite passes through to the atomic UPDATE which fails and throws 409 ConflictException. Should throw 404 NotFoundException before the transaction.

**Issue 3** (major): Clear stale invites on refetch — valid. If `ipnsName` changes or fetch fails, old invites linger in state.

> AGENT

All done. Here's a summary of what was fixed:

1. **`@ApiResponse(404)` on `getInviteStatus`** — Swagger now documents the 404 thrown by `NotFoundException`

2. **Expired invites return 404 instead of 409** — Added pre-transaction checks in `claimInvite` that catch expired and non-active invites *before* entering the transaction. This prevents the atomic UPDATE from throwing 409 (which would signal the token exists). Expired active invites are also cleaned up. Added 3 unit tests.

3. **Clear stale invites on refetch** — `InviteLinkTab` now resets `invites` to `[]` and clears errors at fetch start, plus clears invites on error, preventing stale data from being displayed or revoked.

All tests pass: 578 unit tests, coverage thresholds met, 21/21 E2E tests green. All 3 threads replied to and resolved.
